  "content": "# Live Cross-Conversation Streaming\n\n## Problem\n\nWhen viewing a background-streaming conversation, users see a frozen \"thinking\" state rather than live text appearing character-by-character. The final result only appears once the stream completes and the user switches away and back. This is because the SSE callbacks are tied to one active stream via a generation counter, and switching conversations silences them.\n\n## Requirements\n\n- When switching back to a conversation that's still streaming, live text resumes immediately (re-attachment)\n- Multiple simultaneous streams supported (send in Conv A, then send in Conv B while A streams)\n- Sidebar shows a pulsing dot next to conversations with active background streams\n- No backend changes required\n\n## Approach: Per-Conversation Stream Manager\n\nReplace the singleton refs (`assistantIdRef`, `segmentsRef`, `textBufferRef`, etc.) with a `Map<conversationId, StreamState>`. Each active stream gets its own isolated state. When the user switches conversations, the UI subscribes to whichever conversation's stream state is active.\n\n## Design\n\n### 1. StreamState Type\n\n```typescript\ninterface StreamState {\n  assistantId: string;\n  segments: ContentSegment[];\n  currentText: string;\n  textBuffer: string;\n  phase: 'thinking' | 'answer';\n  abortController: AbortController;\n  sessionId: string | null;\n  messages: ChatMessage[];       // live-updated by callbacks\n  flushTimer: ReturnType<typeof setTimeout> | null;\n}\n```\n\n### 2. Key Data Structures in AgentContext\n\n- `streamStatesRef = useRef<Map<string, StreamState>>(new Map())` — active streams\n- `activeConversationIdRef` — which conversation is being viewed (unchanged)\n- `messagesCacheRef` — cache for non-streaming conversations (unchanged)\n- `streamingConversationIds: Set<string>` — React state for sidebar indicator\n- Remove: `streamGenerationRef`, `assistantIdRef`, `segmentsRef`, `currentTextRef`, `phaseRef`, `textBufferRef`, `flushTimerRef`, `streamConversationIdRef` (all absorbed into StreamState)\n\n### 3. Callback Isolation\n\nEach `sendMessage` creates a `StreamState` and passes callbacks that close over it:\n\n```\ncallback(data) {\n  // Always update the stream's own state\n  streamState.messages = updatedMessages;\n  streamState.segments = updatedSegments;\n  // Only push to React state if this conversation is being viewed\n  if (activeConversationIdRef.current === conversationId) {\n    setMessages(streamState.messages);\n  }\n}\n```\n\nNo generation counter needed — callbacks write to their own StreamState, never to shared refs.\n\n### 4. Conversation Switching (Re-attachment)\n\nWhen user switches FROM A TO B:\n\n1. Snapshot A's `messages` state into `streamStatesRef.get(A).messages` (if streaming) or `messagesCacheRef` (if not)\n2. Check if B has an active stream in `streamStatesRef`:\n   - **Yes (re-attach):** Read `streamStatesRef.get(B).messages`, set as React `messages` state. Future callbacks for B also call `setMessages` since B is now active. Live text resumes immediately.\n   - **No:** Fetch from `/api/conversations/B` or use `messagesCacheRef`.\n3. Update `activeConversationIdRef` to B.\n\n### 5. Multiple Simultaneous Streams\n\n- Remove global `if (isStreaming) return;` guard from `sendMessage`\n- Add per-conversation guard: `if (conversationId && streamStatesRef.current.has(conversationId)) return;`\n- `isStreaming` exposed to ChatInput becomes: does the active conversation have an entry in `streamStatesRef`?\n- `flushText` becomes `flushTextForStream(state: StreamState)` — operates on per-stream state\n\n### 6. Stream Completion (onDone / onError)\n\n1. Remove conversationId from `streamStatesRef` and `streamingConversationIds`\n2. If user is viewing this conversation: update `setMessages` with final state, clear streaming indicators\n3. If user is NOT viewing: backend has persisted the response. Delete stale cache so next switch fetches fresh data.\n\n### 7. Sidebar Streaming Indicator\n\n- New React state in AgentContext: `streamingConversationIds: Set<string>`\n- Passed through `Sidebar` -> `ConversationHistory` as prop\n- Visual: pulsing dot next to conversation title via CSS animation\n- Updated on stream start (add) and stream end (remove)\n\n### 8. editMessage and respondToQuestion\n\n- `editMessage`: Creates a new StreamState (aborts any existing one for that conversation first)\n- `respondToQuestion`: Accepts `conversationId` parameter to look up correct StreamState\n- `deleteMessage`: Checks per-conversation streaming instead of global flag\n\n## Files Changed\n\n- `frontend/src/contexts/AgentContext.tsx` — major refactor (StreamState map, per-stream callbacks, re-attachment)\n- `frontend/src/hooks/useAgent.ts` — add `streamingConversationIds` to context interface\n- `frontend/src/components/ChatInput.tsx` — per-conversation `isStreaming` check\n- `frontend/src/components/ConversationHistory.tsx` — streaming dot indicator\n- `frontend/src/components/ConversationHistory.css` — pulse animation\n- `frontend/src/components/Sidebar.tsx` — thread `streamingConversationIds` prop\n- `frontend/src/App.tsx` — thread `streamingConversationIds` prop\n\n## No Backend Changes\n\nThe backend already:\n- Persists the complete response in the `finally` block (even if client disconnects)\n- Doesn't track streaming state per conversation\n- Supports concurrent SSE connections\n"
}
```

    21→function findDuplicateFileNames(files: File[]): string[] {
    22→  const seen = new Set<string>();
    23→  const duplicates = new Set<string>();
    24→  for (const file of files) {
    25→    if (seen.has(file.name)) {
    26→      duplicates.add(file.name);
    27→    }
    28→    seen.add(file.name);
    29→  }
    30→  return Array.from(duplicates);
    31→}

   375→
   376→export default function App() {
   377→  const sessionId = useSessionId();
   378→  const [tables, setTables] = useState<TableInfo[]>([]);
   379→  const [loading, setLoading] = useState(true);
   380→  const [error, setError] = useState<string | null>(null);
   381→  const refreshTables = useCallback(async () => {
   382→    try {
   383→      const response = await fetch('/api/tables', {
   384→        headers: { 'X-Session-ID': sessionId },
   385→      });
   386→      if (!response.ok) throw new Error('Failed to fetch tables');
   387→      const data = await response.json();
   388→      setTables(data);
   389→    } catch (e) {
   390→      console.error('Failed to refresh tables:', e);
   391→    }
   392→  }, [sessionId]);
   393→
   394→  useEffect(() => {
   395→    (async () => {
   396→      try {
   397→        const response = await fetch('/api/health');
   398→        if (!response.ok) throw new Error('Backend is not available');
   399→        await refreshTables();
   400→      } catch (e) {
   401→        setError(e instanceof Error ? e.message : 'Failed to connect to backend');
   402→      } finally {
   403→        setLoading(false);
   404→      }
   405→    })();
   406→  }, [refreshTables]);
   407→
   408→  if (loading) {
   409→    return <div className="app-loading">Connecting to backend...</div>;
   410→  }
   411→
   412→  if (error) {
   413→    return (
   414→      <div className="app-error">Failed to connect: {error}</div>
   415→    );
   416→  }
   417→
   418→  return (
   419→    <ConfigProvider>
   420→      <LanguageProvider>
   421→        <ThemeProvider>
   422→          <ConversationProvider sessionId={sessionId}>
   423→            <AgentProvider refreshTables={refreshTables}>
   424→              <AppContent tables={tables} refreshTables={refreshTables} sessionId={sessionId} />
   425→            </AgentProvider>
   426→          </ConversationProvider>
   427→        </ThemeProvider>
   428→      </LanguageProvider>
   429→    </ConfigProvider>
   430→  );
   431→}
   432→

{
  "file_path": "/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/.claude/worktrees/implement-memory/docs/plans/2026-03-01-live-cross-conversation-streaming.md",
  "content": "# Live Cross-Conversation Streaming Implementation Plan\n\n> **For Claude:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task.\n\n**Goal:** Enable live re-attachment to background-streaming conversations, support multiple simultaneous streams, and show sidebar streaming indicators.\n\n**Architecture:** Replace singleton streaming refs in AgentContext with a per-conversation `StreamState` map. SSE callbacks write to their own conversation's state. When the user switches conversations, the UI reads from the target conversation's StreamState (if streaming) for instant re-attachment. A React state `streamingConversationIds` tracks which conversations have active streams for sidebar indicators.\n\n**Tech Stack:** React 18, TypeScript, Vite (frontend only — no backend changes)\n\n---\n\n### Task 1: Add StreamState type and streamStatesRef\n\n**Files:**\n- Modify: `frontend/src/contexts/AgentContext.tsx:1-51`\n\n**Step 1: Add StreamState interface and replace singleton refs**\n\nAdd the `StreamState` interface after the existing imports. Replace the singleton refs (`assistantIdRef`, `segmentsRef`, `currentTextRef`, `textBufferRef`, `flushTimerRef`, `phaseRef`, `streamConversationIdRef`, `streamGenerationRef`) with a single `streamStatesRef` map and a `streamingConversationIds` state.\n\nIn `frontend/src/contexts/AgentContext.tsx`, after the imports add:\n\n```typescript\ninterface StreamState {\n  assistantId: string;\n  segments: ContentSegment[];\n  currentText: string;\n  textBuffer: string;\n  phase: 'thinking' | 'answer';\n  abortController: AbortController;\n  sessionId: string | null;\n  messages: ChatMessage[];\n  flushTimer: ReturnType<typeof setTimeout> | null;\n}\n```\n\nThen inside `AgentProvider`, replace the singleton refs:\n\nRemove these refs:\n- `textBufferRef` (line 30)\n- `flushTimerRef` (line 31)\n- `assistantIdRef` (line 32)\n- `segmentsRef` (line 33)\n- `currentTextRef` (line 34)\n- `phaseRef` (line 35)\n- `streamConversationIdRef` (line 47)\n- `streamGenerationRef` (line 51)\n\nAdd:\n```typescript\nconst streamStatesRef = useRef<Map<string, StreamState>>(new Map());\nconst [streamingConversationIds, setStreamingConversationIds] = useState<Set<string>>(new Set());\n```\n\nKeep: `abortRef` (remove later in Task 2), `sessionIdRef`, `pendingHistoryRef`, `messagesRef`, `messagesCacheRef`, `activeConversationIdRef`.\n\n**Step 2: Build the project to verify no runtime errors from type addition**\n\nRun: `cd frontend && npx tsc --noEmit 2>&1 | head -30`\nExpected: Type errors from references to removed refs (expected — we fix those in subsequent tasks)\n\n**Step 3: Commit**\n\n```bash\ngit add frontend/src/contexts/AgentContext.tsx\ngit commit -m \"feat: add StreamState type and streamStatesRef map\"\n```\n\n---\n\n### Task 2: Refactor flushText to be per-stream\n\n**Files:**\n- Modify: `frontend/src/contexts/AgentContext.tsx:53-64`\n\n**Step 1: Replace flushText with flushTextForStream**\n\nReplace the existing `flushText` callback (lines 53-64) with a function that operates on a specific `StreamState`:\n\n```typescript\nconst flushTextForStream = useCallback((state: StreamState, conversationId: string) => {\n  const text = state.textBuffer;\n  if (!text) return;\n  state.currentText += text;\n  state.textBuffer = '';\n\n  // Update the stream's own messages\n  state.messages = state.messages.map((m) =>\n    m.id === state.assistantId ? { ...m, content: m.content + text } : m\n  );\n\n  // If this conversation is currently being viewed, update React state\n  if (activeConversationIdRef.current === conversationId) {\n    setMessages(state.messages);\n  }\n}, []);\n```\n\n**Step 2: Commit**\n\n```bash\ngit add frontend/src/contexts/AgentContext.tsx\ngit commit -m \"refactor: make flushText per-stream with StreamState parameter\"\n```\n\n---\n\n### Task 3: Refactor sendMessage to use per-conversation StreamState\n\n**Files:**\n- Modify: `frontend/src/contexts/AgentContext.tsx:66-384`\n\nThis is the largest task. Replace all the singleton-ref-based callback logic in `sendMessage` with per-stream `StreamState` callbacks.\n\n**Step 1: Rewrite sendMessage**\n\nKey changes:\n1. Remove global `if (isStreaming) return;` guard.\n2. Add per-conversation guard: `if (conversationId && streamStatesRef.current.has(conversationId)) return;`\n3. Create a `StreamState` object for this conversation and put it in `streamStatesRef`.\n4. Add conversationId to `streamingConversationIds`.\n5. All callbacks close over the `StreamState` object and `conversationId`, not singleton refs.\n6. Each callback updates `state.messages` always, and `setMessages(state.messages)` only when `activeConversationIdRef.current === conversationId`.\n7. `onDone` / `onError` remove the StreamState from the map and remove from `streamingConversationIds`.\n\nThe complete rewritten `sendMessage`:\n\n```typescript\nconst sendMessage = useCallback(\n  async (text: string, conversationId?: string | null) => {\n    const convId = conversationId || null;\n    // Per-conversation guard: don't double-send in same conversation\n    if (convId && streamStatesRef.current.has(convId)) return;\n\n    const controller = new AbortController();\n    const assistantId = generateId();\n\n    const userMsg: ChatMessage = {\n      id: generateId(),\n      role: 'user',\n      content: text,\n    };\n    const assistantMsg: ChatMessage = {\n      id: assistantId,\n      role: 'assistant',\n      content: '',\n      toolCalls: [],\n      isStreaming: true,\n    };\n\n    // Build conversation history from current messages or cached messages\n    const currentMsgs = convId && activeConversationIdRef.current === convId\n      ? messagesRef.current\n      : (convId ? messagesCacheRef.current.get(convId) : messagesRef.current) || messagesRef.current;\n    const history = currentMsgs\n      .filter((m) => m.role === 'user' || m.role === 'assistant')\n      .map((m) => ({ role: m.role, content: m.content }));\n\n    const streamMessages = [...currentMsgs, userMsg, assistantMsg];\n\n    // Create per-conversation stream state\n    const state: StreamState = {\n      assistantId,\n      segments: [],\n      currentText: '',\n      textBuffer: '',\n      phase: 'thinking',\n      abortController: controller,\n      sessionId: sessionIdRef.current,\n      messages: streamMessages,\n      flushTimer: null,\n    };\n\n    if (convId) {\n      streamStatesRef.current.set(convId, state);\n      setStreamingConversationIds(prev => new Set(prev).add(convId));\n    }\n\n    // If this is the active conversation, update React state\n    if (activeConversationIdRef.current === convId) {\n      setMessages(streamMessages);\n      setIsStreaming(true);\n    }\n\n    // If there's pending history from a delete, start a new Langfuse session\n    const pendingHistory = pendingHistoryRef.current;\n    pendingHistoryRef.current = null;\n    const langfuseSessionId = pendingHistory ? generateUUID() : null;\n\n    // Extract skill references\n    let actualMessage = text;\n    let skills: string[] | undefined;\n    const slashMatches = [...text.matchAll(/\\/([a-z0-9-]+)/g)];\n    if (slashMatches.length > 0) {\n      skills = slashMatches.map((m) => m[1]);\n      actualMessage = slashMatches.reduce((msg, m) => msg.replace(m[0], ''), text).trim() || text;\n    }\n\n    // Helper to update stream messages and optionally React state\n    const updateMessages = (updater: (msgs: ChatMessage[]) => ChatMessage[]) => {\n      state.messages = updater(state.messages);\n      if (activeConversationIdRef.current === convId) {\n        setMessages(state.messages);\n      }\n    };\n\n    // Helper to schedule text flush\n    const scheduleFlush = () => {\n      if (!state.flushTimer) {\n        state.flushTimer = setTimeout(() => {\n          flushTextForStream(state, convId!);\n          state.flushTimer = null;\n        }, 50);\n      }\n    };\n\n    // Helper to flush pending text immediately\n    const flushNow = () => {\n      if (state.flushTimer) {\n        clearTimeout(state.flushTimer);\n        state.flushTimer = null;\n      }\n      flushTextForStream(state, convId!);\n    };\n\n    // Helper to finalize stream (called by onDone and onError)\n    const cleanupStream = () => {\n      if (state.flushTimer) {\n        clearTimeout(state.flushTimer);\n        state.flushTimer = null;\n      }\n      if (convId) {\n        streamStatesRef.current.delete(convId);\n        setStreamingConversationIds(prev => {\n          const next = new Set(prev);\n          next.delete(convId);\n          return next;\n        });\n      }\n    };\n\n    await runAgentLoop(\n      actualMessage,\n      state.sessionId,\n      langfuseSessionId,\n      pendingHistory ?? (history.length > 0 ? history : null),\n      conversationId,\n      {\n        onTextChunk: (chunk) => {\n          state.textBuffer += chunk;\n          scheduleFlush();\n        },\n        onThinkingDone: () => {\n          flushNow();\n          if (state.currentText.trim()) {\n            state.segments.push({ type: 'thinking', text: state.currentText });\n            state.currentText = '';\n          }\n          state.phase = 'answer';\n          updateMessages(msgs =>\n            msgs.map(m =>\n              m.id === assistantId\n                ? { ...m, currentPhase: 'answer', segments: [...state.segments] }\n                : m\n            )\n          );\n        },\n        onToolCall: (pending: ToolCallResult) => {\n          flushNow();\n          if (state.currentText.trim()) {\n            const segType = state.phase === 'answer' ? 'answer' : 'thinking';\n            state.segments.push({ type: segType, text: state.currentText });\n            state.currentText = '';\n          }\n          state.segments.push({ type: 'tool', toolResult: pending });\n          updateMessages(msgs =>\n            msgs.map(m =>\n              m.id === assistantId\n                ? { ...m, currentPhase: 'answer', segments: [...state.segments] }\n                : m\n            )\n          );\n        },\n        onToolResult: (result: ToolCallResult) => {\n          const pendingIdx = state.segments.findIndex(\n            s => s.type === 'tool' && s.toolResult?.toolCallId === result.toolCallId\n          );\n          if (pendingIdx !== -1) {\n            const pending = state.segments[pendingIdx].toolResult!;\n            state.segments[pendingIdx] = {\n              type: 'tool',\n              toolResult: {\n                ...pending,\n                ...result,\n                sql: result.sql || pending.sql,\n                toolName: result.toolName || pending.toolName,\n                command: result.command || pending.command,\n                toolInput: result.toolInput || pending.toolInput,\n              },\n            };\n          } else {\n            state.segments.push({ type: 'tool', toolResult: result });\n          }\n          updateMessages(msgs =>\n            msgs.map(m =>\n              m.id === assistantId\n                ? { ...m, toolCalls: [...(m.toolCalls || []), result], segments: [...state.segments] }\n                : m\n            )\n          );\n          refreshTables();\n          if (result.toolName?.includes('create_skill')) {\n            window.dispatchEvent(new CustomEvent('skills-updated'));\n          }\n          if (result.toolName?.includes('save_memory') || result.toolName?.includes('forget_memory')) {\n            window.dispatchEvent(new CustomEvent('memories-updated'));\n          }\n        },\n        onSubagentStart: (data) => {\n          flushNow();\n          if (state.currentText.trim()) {\n            const segType = state.phase === 'answer' ? 'answer' : 'thinking';\n            state.segments.push({ type: segType, text: state.currentText });\n            state.currentText = '';\n          }\n          state.segments.push({\n            type: 'subagent_start',\n            subagentId: data.id,\n            subagentName: data.name,\n            text: data.prompt,\n          });\n          updateMessages(msgs =>\n            msgs.map(m =>\n              m.id === assistantId\n                ? { ...m, segments: [...state.segments] }\n                : m\n            )\n          );\n        },\n        onSubagentEnd: (data) => {\n          state.segments.push({\n            type: 'subagent_end',\n            subagentId: data.id,\n            subagentName: data.name,\n            chart_spec: data.chart_spec,\n            sqlResults: data.sql_results,\n            text: data.result,\n          });\n          updateMessages(msgs =>\n            msgs.map(m =>\n              m.id === assistantId\n                ? { ...m, segments: [...state.segments] }\n                : m\n            )\n          );\n        },\n        onDone: (newSessionId) => {\n          if (newSessionId) {\n            state.sessionId = newSessionId;\n            sessionIdRef.current = newSessionId;\n          }\n          flushNow();\n          if (state.currentText.trim()) {\n            state.segments.push({ type: 'answer', text: state.currentText });\n            state.currentText = '';\n          }\n          updateMessages(msgs =>\n            msgs.map(m =>\n              m.id === assistantId\n                ? { ...m, isStreaming: false, currentPhase: undefined, segments: [...state.segments] }\n                : m\n            )\n          );\n          cleanupStream();\n          if (activeConversationIdRef.current === convId) {\n            setIsStreaming(false);\n          } else if (convId) {\n            // Cache final messages so switching back shows completed response\n            messagesCacheRef.current.set(convId, state.messages);\n          }\n        },\n        onError: (error) => {\n          flushNow();\n          if (state.currentText.trim()) {\n            const segType = state.phase === 'answer' ? 'answer' : 'thinking';\n            state.segments.push({ type: segType, text: state.currentText });\n            state.currentText = '';\n          }\n          state.segments.push({ type: 'error', errorMessage: error });\n          updateMessages(msgs =>\n            msgs.map(m =>\n              m.id === assistantId\n                ? { ...m, isStreaming: false, currentPhase: undefined, segments: [...state.segments] }\n                : m\n            )\n          );\n          cleanupStream();\n          if (activeConversationIdRef.current === convId) {\n            setIsStreaming(false);\n          } else if (convId) {\n            messagesCacheRef.current.set(convId, state.messages);\n          }\n        },\n        onUserQuestion: (data) => {\n          flushNow();\n          if (state.currentText.trim()) {\n            const segType = state.phase === 'answer' ? 'answer' : 'thinking';\n            state.segments.push({ type: segType, text: state.currentText });\n            state.currentText = '';\n          }\n          state.segments.push({ type: 'user_question', questionData: data });\n          updateMessages(msgs =>\n            msgs.map(m =>\n              m.id === assistantId\n                ? { ...m, segments: [...state.segments] }\n                : m\n            )\n          );\n        },\n      },\n      controller.signal,\n      userSessionId,\n      skills,\n    );\n  },\n  [flushTextForStream, refreshTables, userSessionId]\n);\n```\n\n**Step 2: Remove `abortRef` (no longer used by sendMessage)**\n\nThe `abortRef` is now stored per-stream in `StreamState.abortController`. Remove the `abortRef` declaration (line 29). If `editMessage` still uses it, keep it temporarily (handled in Task 5).\n\n**Step 3: Verify compilation**\n\nRun: `cd frontend && npx tsc --noEmit 2>&1 | head -30`\nExpected: Errors from `editMessage`, `loadMessages`, `clearMessages` still referencing old refs (fixed in Tasks 4-5)\n\n**Step 4: Commit**\n\n```bash\ngit add frontend/src/contexts/AgentContext.tsx\ngit commit -m \"feat: rewrite sendMessage with per-conversation StreamState\"\n```\n\n---\n\n### Task 4: Refactor loadMessages and clearMessages for re-attachment\n\n**Files:**\n- Modify: `frontend/src/contexts/AgentContext.tsx` (loadMessages ~720-811, clearMessages ~813-874)\n\n**Step 1: Rewrite loadMessages**\n\nThe key change: when switching to a conversation that has an active stream in `streamStatesRef`, read its `state.messages` directly (re-attachment). No need to increment a generation counter.\n\n```typescript\nconst loadMessages = useCallback((msgs: ChatMessage[], outgoingConversationId?: string | null, incomingConversationId?: string | null) => {\n  const outgoing = outgoingConversationId || null;\n  const incoming = incomingConversationId || null;\n  activeConversationIdRef.current = incoming;\n\n  // Save outgoing conversation's messages to cache (only if not streaming —\n  // streaming conversations keep their state in streamStatesRef)\n  if (outgoing && !streamStatesRef.current.has(outgoing)) {\n    const currentMsgs = messagesRef.current;\n    if (currentMsgs.length > 0) {\n      messagesCacheRef.current.set(outgoing, currentMsgs);\n    }\n  }\n\n  // Check if incoming conversation has an active stream (re-attachment)\n  if (incoming) {\n    const streamState = streamStatesRef.current.get(incoming);\n    if (streamState) {\n      // Re-attach: use live stream messages\n      setMessages(streamState.messages);\n      setIsStreaming(true);\n      return;\n    }\n  }\n\n  // No active stream — use backend data or cache\n  let finalMsgs = msgs;\n  if (incoming) {\n    const cached = messagesCacheRef.current.get(incoming);\n    if (cached && cached.length > 0 && cached.length > msgs.length) {\n      finalMsgs = cached;\n    }\n    messagesCacheRef.current.delete(incoming);\n  }\n\n  setMessages(finalMsgs);\n  const hasStreamingMsg = finalMsgs.some(m => m.isStreaming);\n  setIsStreaming(hasStreamingMsg);\n  sessionIdRef.current = null;\n  pendingHistoryRef.current = null;\n}, []);\n```\n\n**Step 2: Rewrite clearMessages**\n\n```typescript\nconst clearMessages = useCallback((outgoingConversationId?: string | null) => {\n  const outgoing = outgoingConversationId || null;\n  activeConversationIdRef.current = null;\n\n  // Save outgoing messages to cache if not streaming\n  if (outgoing && !streamStatesRef.current.has(outgoing)) {\n    const currentMsgs = messagesRef.current;\n    if (currentMsgs.length > 0) {\n      messagesCacheRef.current.set(outgoing, currentMsgs);\n    }\n  }\n\n  setMessages([]);\n  setIsStreaming(false);\n  sessionIdRef.current = null;\n  pendingHistoryRef.current = null;\n}, []);\n```\n\n**Step 3: Verify compilation**\n\nRun: `cd frontend && npx tsc --noEmit 2>&1 | head -30`\nExpected: Errors only from `editMessage` and `respondToQuestion` (fixed in Task 5)\n\n**Step 4: Commit**\n\n```bash\ngit add frontend/src/contexts/AgentContext.tsx\ngit commit -m \"feat: rewrite loadMessages/clearMessages with stream re-attachment\"\n```\n\n---\n\n### Task 5: Refactor editMessage, deleteMessage, respondToQuestion\n\n**Files:**\n- Modify: `frontend/src/contexts/AgentContext.tsx` (editMessage ~386-645, deleteMessage ~647-676, respondToQuestion ~678-718)\n\n**Step 1: Refactor editMessage**\n\n`editMessage` needs to:\n1. Abort any existing stream for the active conversation.\n2. Create a new `StreamState` for the edit.\n3. Use the same per-stream callback pattern as `sendMessage`.\n\nThe structure mirrors `sendMessage` but uses `runAgentEditLoop` and truncates messages first. Apply the same pattern: create `StreamState`, close callbacks over it, use `updateMessages` helper.\n\nKey changes vs current code:\n- Get `convId` from `activeConversationIdRef.current`\n- If `convId` has an active stream, abort it first: `streamStatesRef.current.get(convId)?.abortController.abort()`; then delete from map\n- Create new `StreamState` with truncated messages\n- Use same callback pattern as sendMessage (close over `state` and `convId`)\n\n**Step 2: Refactor respondToQuestion**\n\nAdd `conversationId` parameter. Look up the stream state from `streamStatesRef.current.get(conversationId)` to find the correct `segments` and `assistantId`:\n\n```typescript\nconst respondToQuestion = useCallback(\n  async (questionId: string, answers: string[], freeText?: string, conversationId?: string | null) => {\n    // Find the stream state that contains this question\n    let targetState: StreamState | undefined;\n    let targetConvId: string | null = null;\n    if (conversationId) {\n      targetState = streamStatesRef.current.get(conversationId);\n      targetConvId = conversationId;\n    }\n    if (!targetState) {\n      // Search all active streams\n      for (const [cid, state] of streamStatesRef.current) {\n        if (state.segments.some(s => s.type === 'user_question' && s.questionData?.questionId === questionId)) {\n          targetState = state;\n          targetConvId = cid;\n          break;\n        }\n      }\n    }\n\n    if (targetState) {\n      const segIdx = targetState.segments.findIndex(\n        s => s.type === 'user_question' && s.questionData?.questionId === questionId\n      );\n      if (segIdx !== -1) {\n        targetState.segments[segIdx] = {\n          ...targetState.segments[segIdx],\n          userAnswer: answers,\n          userFreeText: freeText,\n        };\n        targetState.messages = targetState.messages.map(m =>\n          m.id === targetState!.assistantId\n            ? { ...m, segments: [...targetState!.segments] }\n            : m\n        );\n        if (activeConversationIdRef.current === targetConvId) {\n          setMessages(targetState.messages);\n        }\n      }\n    }\n\n    // POST to backend (unchanged)\n    try {\n      await fetch('/api/chat/respond', {\n        method: 'POST',\n        headers: {\n          'Content-Type': 'application/json',\n          ...(userSessionId ? { 'X-Session-ID': userSessionId } : {}),\n        },\n        body: JSON.stringify({\n          question_id: questionId,\n          answers,\n          free_text: freeText || null,\n        }),\n      });\n    } catch (e) {\n      console.error('Failed to respond to question:', e);\n    }\n  },\n  [userSessionId]\n);\n```\n\n**Step 3: Refactor deleteMessage**\n\nChange guard from `if (isStreaming) return;` to check per-conversation:\n\n```typescript\nconst deleteMessage = useCallback(\n  (messageIndex: number) => {\n    const convId = activeConversationIdRef.current;\n    // Only block if this specific conversation is streaming\n    if (convId && streamStatesRef.current.has(convId)) return;\n    // ... rest unchanged\n  },\n  []\n);\n```\n\n**Step 4: Remove old singleton refs that are no longer referenced**\n\nRemove `abortRef` if not used anywhere else. Verify no references to `streamGenerationRef`, `streamConversationIdRef`, `assistantIdRef`, `segmentsRef`, `currentTextRef`, `textBufferRef`, `flushTimerRef`, `phaseRef` remain.\n\n**Step 5: Verify compilation**\n\nRun: `cd frontend && npx tsc --noEmit 2>&1 | head -30`\nExpected: PASS (or only warnings)\n\n**Step 6: Commit**\n\n```bash\ngit add frontend/src/contexts/AgentContext.tsx\ngit commit -m \"feat: refactor editMessage/deleteMessage/respondToQuestion for per-stream state\"\n```\n\n---\n\n### Task 6: Expose streamingConversationIds in context and update ChatInput\n\n**Files:**\n- Modify: `frontend/src/hooks/useAgent.ts:4-13` — add `streamingConversationIds` to interface\n- Modify: `frontend/src/contexts/AgentContext.tsx:876-883` — pass in provider value\n- Modify: `frontend/src/components/ChatInput.tsx:17,49,140-145,151` — per-conversation isStreaming\n\n**Step 1: Update AgentContextValue interface**\n\nIn `frontend/src/hooks/useAgent.ts`, add to the interface:\n\n```typescript\nstreamingConversationIds: Set<string>;\n```\n\nAnd update the default context value:\n\n```typescript\nstreamingConversationIds: new Set(),\n```\n\n**Step 2: Pass streamingConversationIds in AgentContext.Provider**\n\nIn `frontend/src/contexts/AgentContext.tsx`, add `streamingConversationIds` to the provider value object.\n\n**Step 3: Update ChatInput to use per-conversation streaming**\n\nIn `frontend/src/components/ChatInput.tsx`:\n\nReplace:\n```typescript\nconst { sendMessage, isStreaming } = useAgent();\n```\n\nWith:\n```typescript\nconst { sendMessage, isStreaming, streamingConversationIds } = useAgent();\n```\n\nThe existing `isStreaming` still works for the active conversation (set by `sendMessage` and `loadMessages`). But the send guard should also check:\n\n```typescript\nconst handleSend = async () => {\n  const trimmed = text.trim();\n  if (!trimmed || isStreaming) return;\n  // ... rest unchanged\n};\n```\n\nThis already works because `isStreaming` is set to true when the active conversation is streaming, and re-attachment sets it too.\n\n**Step 4: Verify compilation**\n\nRun: `cd frontend && npx tsc --noEmit 2>&1 | head -30`\nExpected: PASS\n\n**Step 5: Commit**\n\n```bash\ngit add frontend/src/hooks/useAgent.ts frontend/src/contexts/AgentContext.tsx frontend/src/components/ChatInput.tsx\ngit commit -m \"feat: expose streamingConversationIds in AgentContext\"\n```\n\n---\n\n### Task 7: Add sidebar streaming indicator\n\n**Files:**\n- Modify: `frontend/src/components/Sidebar.tsx:10-26,28,181-189` — add and thread prop\n- Modify: `frontend/src/components/ConversationHistory.tsx:12-19,115-165` — render indicator\n- Modify: `frontend/src/components/ConversationHistory.css` — pulse animation\n- Modify: `frontend/src/App.tsx:54,258-274` — pass prop from context\n\n**Step 1: Thread streamingConversationIds through App -> Sidebar -> ConversationHistory**\n\nIn `frontend/src/App.tsx`, get from agent context:\n\n```typescript\nconst { clearMessages, loadMessages, streamingConversationIds } = useAgent();\n```\n\nPass to Sidebar:\n\n```typescript\n<Sidebar\n  // ... existing props\n  streamingConversationIds={streamingConversationIds}\n/>\n```\n\nIn `frontend/src/components/Sidebar.tsx`, add to `SidebarProps`:\n\n```typescript\nstreamingConversationIds: Set<string>;\n```\n\nThread to ConversationHistory:\n\n```typescript\n<ConversationHistory\n  // ... existing props\n  streamingConversationIds={streamingConversationIds}\n/>\n```\n\n**Step 2: Add streaming dot to ConversationHistory**\n\nIn `frontend/src/components/ConversationHistory.tsx`, add to `ConversationHistoryProps`:\n\n```typescript\nstreamingConversationIds: Set<string>;\n```\n\nIn the conversation item render, add a streaming dot next to the title:\n\n```tsx\n<div className=\"conv-history__item-title\">\n  {streamingConversationIds.has(conv.id) && (\n    <span className=\"conv-history__streaming-dot\" />\n  )}\n  {conv.title || t('untitled')}\n</div>\n```\n\n**Step 3: Add CSS animation**\n\nIn `frontend/src/components/ConversationHistory.css`, add:\n\n```css\n.conv-history__streaming-dot {\n  display: inline-block;\n  width: 6px;\n  height: 6px;\n  border-radius: 50%;\n  background: var(--accent-color, #3b82f6);\n  margin-right: 6px;\n  vertical-align: middle;\n  flex-shrink: 0;\n  animation: conv-streaming-pulse 1.5s ease-in-out infinite;\n}\n\n@keyframes conv-streaming-pulse {\n  0%, 100% { opacity: 1; }\n  50% { opacity: 0.3; }\n}\n```\n\n**Step 4: Verify compilation**\n\nRun: `cd frontend && npx tsc --noEmit      1→# Live Cross-Conversation Streaming Implementation Plan
     2→
     3→> **For Claude:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task.
     4→
     5→**Goal:** Enable live re-attachment to background-streaming conversations, support multiple simultaneous streams, and show sidebar streaming indicators.
     6→
     7→**Architecture:** Replace singleton streaming refs in AgentContext with a per-conversation `StreamState` map. SSE callbacks write to their own conversation's state. When the user switches conversations, the UI reads from the target conversation's StreamState (if streaming) for instant re-attachment. A React state `streamingConversationIds` tracks which conversations have active streams for sidebar indicators.

   109→### Task 3: Refactor sendMessage to use per-conversation StreamState
   110→
   111→**Files:**
   112→- Modify: `frontend/src/contexts/AgentContext.tsx:66-384`
   113→
   114→This is the largest task. Replace all the singleton-ref-based callback logic in `sendMessage` with per-stream `StreamState` callbacks.
   115→
   116→**Step 1: Rewrite sendMessage**
   117→
   118→Key changes:
   119→1. Remove global `if (isStreaming) return;` guard.
   120→2. Add per-conversation guard: `if (conversationId && streamStatesRef.current.has(conversationId)) return;`
   121→3. Create a `StreamState` object for this conversation and put it in `streamStatesRef`.
   122→4. Add conversationId to `streamingConversationIds`.
   123→5. All callbacks close over the `StreamState` object and `conversationId`, not singleton refs.
   124→6. Each callback updates `state.messages` always, and `setMessages(state.messages)` only when `activeConversationIdRef.current === conversationId`.
   125→7. `onDone` / `onError` remove the StreamState from the map and remove from `streamingConversationIds`.
   126→
   127→The complete rewritten `sendMessage`:
   128→
   129→```typescript
   130→const sendMessage = useCallback(
   131→  async (text: string, conversationId?: string | null) => {
   132→    const convId = conversationId || null;
   133→    // Per-conversation guard: don't double-send in same conversation
   134→    if (convId && streamStatesRef.current.has(convId)) return;
   135→
   136→    const controller = new AbortController();
   137→    const assistantId = generateId();
   138→
   139→    const userMsg: ChatMessage = {
   140→      id: generateId(),
   141→      role: 'user',
   142→      content: text,
   143→    };
   144→    const assistantMsg: ChatMessage = {
   145→      id: assistantId,
   146→      role: 'assistant',
   147→      content: '',
   148→      toolCalls: [],
   149→      isStreaming: true,
   150→    };
   151→
   152→    // Build conversation history from current messages or cached messages
   153→    const currentMsgs = convId && activeConversationIdRef.current === convId
   154→      ? messagesRef.current
   155→      : (convId ? messagesCacheRef.current.get(convId) : messagesRef.current) || messagesRef.current;
   156→    const history = currentMsgs
   157→      .filter((m) => m.role === 'user' || m.role === 'assistant')
   158→      .map((m) => ({ role: m.role, content: m.content }));
   159→
   160→    const streamMessages = [...currentMsgs, userMsg, assistantMsg];
   161→
   162→    // Create per-conversation stream state
   163→    const state: StreamState = {
   164→      assistantId,
   165→      segments: [],
   166→      currentText: '',
   167→      textBuffer: '',
   168→      phase: 'thinking',
   169→      abortController: controller,
   170→      sessionId: sessionIdRef.current,
   171→      messages: streamMessages,
   172→      flushTimer: null,
   173→    };
   174→
   175→    if (convId) {
   176→      streamStatesRef.current.set(convId, state);
   177→      setStreamingConversationIds(prev => new Set(prev).add(convId));
   178→    }
   179→
   180→    // If this is the active conversation, update React state
   181→    if (activeConversationIdRef.current === convId) {
   182→      setMessages(streamMessages);
   183→      setIsStreaming(true);
   184→    }
   185→
   186→    // If there's pending history from a delete, start a new Langfuse session
   187→    const pendingHistory = pendingHistoryRef.current;
   188→    pendingHistoryRef.current = null;
   189→    const langfuseSessionId = pendingHistory ? generateUUID() : null;
   190→
   191→    // Extract skill references
   192→    let actualMessage = text;
   193→    let skills: string[] | undefined;
   194→    const slashMatches = [...text.matchAll(/\/([a-z0-9-]+)/g)];
   195→    if (slashMatches.length > 0) {
   196→      skills = slashMatches.map((m) => m[1]);
   197→      actualMessage = slashMatches.reduce((msg, m) => msg.replace(m[0], ''), text).trim() || text;
   198→    }
   199→
   200→    // Helper to update stream messages and optionally React state
   201→    const updateMessages = (updater: (msgs: ChatMessage[]) => ChatMessage[]) => {
   202→      state.messages = updater(state.messages);
   203→      if (activeConversationIdRef.current === convId) {
   204→        setMessages(state.messages);
   205→      }
   206→    };
   207→
   208→    // Helper to schedule text flush
   209→    const scheduleFlush = () => {
   210→      if (!state.flushTimer) {
   211→        state.flushTimer = setTimeout(() => {
   212→          flushTextForStream(state, convId!);
   213→          state.flushTimer = null;
   214→        }, 50);
   215→      }
   216→    };
   217→
   218→    // Helper to flush pending text immediately
   219→    const flushNow = () => {
   220→      if (state.flushTimer) {
   221→        clearTimeout(state.flushTimer);
   222→        state.flushTimer = null;
   223→      }
   224→      flushTextForStream(state, convId!);
   225→    };
   226→
   227→    // Helper to finalize stream (called by onDone and onError)
   228→    const cleanupStream = () => {
   229→      if (state.flushTimer) {
   230→        clearTimeout(state.flushTimer);
   231→        state.flushTimer = null;
   232→      }
   233→      if (convId) {
   234→        streamStatesRef.current.delete(convId);
   235→        setStreamingConversationIds(prev => {
   236→          const next = new Set(prev);
   237→          next.delete(convId);
   238→          return next;
   239→        });
   240→      }
   241→    };
   242→
   243→    await runAgentLoop(
   244→      actualMessage,
   245→      state.sessionId,
   246→      langfuseSessionId,
   247→      pendingHistory ?? (history.length > 0 ? history : null),
   248→      conversationId,
   249→      {
   250→        onTextChunk: (chunk) => {
   251→          state.textBuffer += chunk;
   252→          scheduleFlush();
   253→        },
   254→        onThinkingDone: () => {
   255→          flushNow();
   256→          if (state.currentText.trim()) {
   257→            state.segments.push({ type: 'thinking', text: state.currentText });
   258→            state.currentText = '';
   259→          }
   260→          state.phase = 'answer';
   261→          updateMessages(msgs =>
   262→            msgs.map(m =>
   263→              m.id === assistantId
   264→                ? { ...m, currentPhase: 'answer', segments: [...state.segments] }
   265→                : m
   266→            )
   267→          );
   268→        },
   269→        onToolCall: (pending: ToolCallResult) => {
   270→          flushNow();
   271→          if (state.currentText.trim()) {
   272→            const segType = state.phase === 'answer' ? 'answer' : 'thinking';
   273→            state.segments.push({ type: segType, text: state.currentText });
   274→            state.currentText = '';
   275→          }
   276→          state.segments.push({ type: 'tool', toolResult: pending });
   277→          updateMessages(msgs =>
   278→            msgs.map(m =>
   279→              m.id === assistantId
   280→                ? { ...m, currentPhase: 'answer', segments: [...state.segments] }
   281→                : m
   282→            )
   283→          );
   284→        },
   285→        onToolResult: (result: ToolCallResult) => {
   286→          const pendingIdx = state.segments.findIndex(
   287→            s => s.type === 'tool' && s.toolResult?.toolCallId === result.toolCallId
   288→          );
   289→          if (pendingIdx !== -1) {
   290→            const pending = state.segments[pendingIdx].toolResult!;
   291→            state.segments[pendingIdx] = {
   292→              type: 'tool',
   293→              toolResult: {
   294→                ...pending,
   295→                ...result,
   296→                sql: result.sql || pending.sql,
   297→                toolName: result.toolName || pending.toolName,
   298→                command: result.command || pending.command,
   299→                toolInput: result.toolInput || pending.toolInput,
   300→              },
   301→            };
   302→          } else {
   303→            state.segments.push({ type: 'tool', toolResult: result });
   304→          }
   305→          updateMessages(msgs =>
   306→            msgs.map(m =>
   307→              m.id === assistantId
   308→                ? { ...m, toolCalls: [...(m.toolCalls || []), result], segments: [...state.segments] }
   309→                : m
   310→            )
   311→          );
   312→          refreshTables();
   313→          if (result.toolName?.includes('create_skill')) {
   314→            window.dispatchEvent(new CustomEvent('skills-updated'));
   315→          }
   316→          if (result.toolName?.includes('save_memory') || result.toolName?.includes('forget_memory')) {
   317→            window.dispatchEvent(new CustomEvent('memories-updated'));
   318→          }
   319→        },
   320→        onSubagentStart: (data) => {
   321→          flushNow();
   322→          if (state.currentText.trim()) {
   323→            const segType = state.phase === 'answer' ? 'answer' : 'thinking';
   324→            state.segments.push({ type: segType, text: state.currentText });
   325→            state.currentText = '';
   326→          }
   327→          state.segments.push({
   328→            type: 'subagent_start',
   329→            subagentId: data.id,
   330→            subagentName: data.name,
   331→            text: data.prompt,
   332→          });
   333→          updateMessages(msgs =>
   334→            msgs.map(m =>
   335→              m.id === assistantId
   336→                ? { ...m, segments: [...state.segments] }
   337→                : m
   338→            )
   339→          );
   340→        },
   341→        onSubagentEnd: (data) => {
   342→          state.segments.push({
   343→            type: 'subagent_end',
   344→            subagentId: data.id,
   345→            subagentName: data.name,
   346→            chart_spec: data.chart_spec,
   347→            sqlResults: data.sql_results,
   348→            text: data.result,
   349→          });
   350→          updateMessages(msgs =>
   351→            msgs.map(m =>
   352→              m.id === assistantId
   353→                ? { ...m, segments: [...state.segments] }
   354→                : m
   355→            )
   356→          );
   357→        },
   358→        onDone: (newSessionId) => {
   359→          if (newSessionId) {
   360→            state.sessionId = newSessionId;
   361→            sessionIdRef.current = newSessionId;
   362→          }
   363→          flushNow();
   364→          if (state.currentText.trim()) {
   365→            state.segments.push({ type: 'answer', text: state.currentText });
   366→            state.currentText = '';
   367→          }
   368→          updateMessages(msgs =>
   369→            msgs.map(m =>
   370→              m.id === assistantId
   371→                ? { ...m, isStreaming: false, currentPhase: undefined, segments: [...state.segments] }
   372→                : m
   373→            )
   374→          );
   375→          cleanupStream();
   376→          if (activeConversationIdRef.current === convId) {
   377→            setIsStreaming(false);
   378→          } else if (convId) {
   379→            // Cache final messages so switching back shows completed response
   380→            messagesCacheRef.current.set(convId, state.messages);
   381→          }
   382→        },
   383→        onError: (error) => {
   384→          flushNow();
   385→          if (state.currentText.trim()) {
   386→            const segType = state.phase === 'answer' ? 'answer' : 'thinking';
   387→            state.segments.push({ type: segType, text: state.currentText });
   388→            state.currentText = '';
   389→          }
   390→          state.segments.push({ type: 'error', errorMessage: error });
   391→          updateMessages(msgs =>
   392→            msgs.map(m =>
   393→              m.id === assistantId
   394→                ? { ...m, isStreaming: false, currentPhase: undefined, segments: [...state.segments] }
   395→                : m
   396→            )
   397→          );
   398→          cleanupStream();
   399→          if (activeConversationIdRef.current === convId) {
   400→            setIsStreaming(false);
   401→          } else if (convId) {
   402→            messagesCacheRef.current.set(convId, state.messages);
   403→          }
   404→        },
   405→        onUserQuestion: (data) => {
   406→          flushNow();
   407→          if (state.currentText.trim()) {
   408→            const segType = state.phase === 'answer' ? 'answer' : 'thinking';
   409→            state.segments.push({ type: segType, text: state.currentText });
   410→            state.currentText = '';
   411→          }
   412→          state.segments.push({ type: 'user_question', questionData: data });
   413→          updateMessages(msgs =>
   414→            msgs.map(m =>
   415→              m.id === assistantId
   416→                ? { ...m, segments: [...state.segments] }
   417→                : m
   418→            )
   419→          );
   420→        },
   421→      },
   422→      controller.signal,
   423→      userSessionId,
   424→      skills,
   425→    );
   426→  },
   427→  [flushTextForStream, refreshTables, userSessionId]
   428→);

   448→
   449→### Task 4: Refactor loadMessages and clearMessages for re-attachment
   450→
   451→**Files:**
   452→- Modify: `frontend/src/contexts/AgentContext.tsx` (loadMessages ~720-811, clearMessages ~813-874)
   453→
   454→**Step 1: Rewrite loadMessages**
   455→
   456→The key change: when switching to a conversation that has an active stream in `streamStatesRef`, read its `state.messages` directly (re-attachment). No need to increment a generation counter.
   457→
   458→```typescript
   459→const loadMessages = useCallback((msgs: ChatMessage[], outgoingConversationId?: string | null, incomingConversationId?: string | null) => {
   460→  const outgoing = outgoingConversationId || null;
   461→  const incoming = incomingConversationId || null;
   462→  activeConversationIdRef.current = incoming;
   463→
   464→  // Save outgoing conversation's messages to cache (only if not streaming —
   465→  // streaming conversations keep their state in streamStatesRef)
   466→  if (outgoing && !streamStatesRef.current.has(outgoing)) {
   467→    const currentMsgs = messagesRef.current;
   468→    if (currentMsgs.length > 0) {
   469→      messagesCacheRef.current.set(outgoing, currentMsgs);
   470→    }
   471→  }
   472→
   473→  // Check if incoming conversation has an active stream (re-attachment)
   474→  if (incoming) {
   475→    const streamState = streamStatesRef.current.get(incoming);
   476→    if (streamState) {
   477→      // Re-attach: use live stream messages
   478→      setMessages(streamState.messages);
   479→      setIsStreaming(true);
   480→      return;
   481→    }
   482→  }
   483→
   484→  // No active stream — use backend data or cache
   485→  let finalMsgs = msgs;
   486→  if (incoming) {
   487→    const cached = messagesCacheRef.current.get(incoming);
   488→    if (cached && cached.length > 0 && cached.length > msgs.length) {
   489→      finalMsgs = cached;
   490→    }
   491→    messagesCacheRef.current.delete(incoming);
   492→  }
   493→
   494→  setMessages(finalMsgs);
   495→  const hasStreamingMsg = finalMsgs.some(m => m.isStreaming);
   496→  setIsStreaming(hasStreamingMsg);
   497→  sessionIdRef.current = null;
   498→  pendingHistoryRef.current = null;
   499→}, []);
   500→```
   501→
   502→**Step 2: Rewrite clearMessages**
   503→
   504→```typescript
   505→const clearMessages = useCallback((outgoingConversationId?: string | null) => {
   506→  const outgoing = outgoingConversationId || null;
   507→  activeConversationIdRef.current = null;
   508→
   509→  // Save outgoing messages to cache if not streaming
   510→  if (outgoing && !streamStatesRef.current.has(outgoing)) {
   511→    const currentMsgs = messagesRef.current;
   512→    if (currentMsgs.length > 0) {
   513→      messagesCacheRef.current.set(outgoing, currentMsgs);
   514→    }
   515→  }
   516→
   517→  setMessages([]);
   518→  setIsStreaming(false);
   519→  sessionIdRef.current = null;
   520→  pendingHistoryRef.current = null;
   521→}, []);
   522→```

   537→
   538→### Task 5: Refactor editMessage, deleteMessage, respondToQuestion
   539→
   540→**Files:**
   541→- Modify: `frontend/src/contexts/AgentContext.tsx` (editMessage ~386-645, deleteMessage ~647-676, respondToQuestion ~678-718)
   542→
   543→**Step 1: Refactor editMessage**
   544→
   545→`editMessage` needs to:
   546→1. Abort any existing stream for the active conversation.
   547→2. Create a new `StreamState` for the edit.
   548→3. Use the same per-stream callback pattern as `sendMessage`.
   549→
   550→The structure mirrors `sendMessage` but uses `runAgentEditLoop` and truncates messages first. Apply the same pattern: create `StreamState`, close callbacks over it, use `updateMessages` helper.
   551→
   552→Key changes vs current code:
   553→- Get `convId` from `activeConversationIdRef.current`
   554→- If `convId` has an active stream, abort it first: `streamStatesRef.current.get(convId)?.abortController.abort()`; then delete from map
   555→- Create new `StreamState` with truncated messages
   556→- Use same callback pattern as sendMessage (close over `state` and `convId`)
   557→
   558→**Step 2: Refactor respondToQuestion**
   559→
   560→Add `conversationId` parameter. Look up the stream state from `streamStatesRef.current.get(conversationId)` to find the correct `segments` and `assistantId`:
   561→
   562→```typescript
   563→const respondToQuestion = useCallback(
   564→  async (questionId: string, answers: string[], freeText?: string, conversationId?: string | null) => {
   565→    // Find the stream state that contains this question
   566→    let targetState: StreamState | undefined;
   567→    let targetConvId: string | null = null;
   568→    if (conversationId) {
   569→      targetState = streamStatesRef.current.get(conversationId);
   570→      targetConvId = conversationId;
   571→    }
   572→    if (!targetState) {
   573→      // Search all active streams
   574→      for (const [cid, state] of streamStatesRef.current) {
   575→        if (state.segments.some(s => s.type === 'user_question' && s.questionData?.questionId === questionId)) {
   576→          targetState = state;
   577→          targetConvId = cid;
   578→          break;
   579→        }
   580→      }
   581→    }
   582→
   583→    if (targetState) {
   584→      const segIdx = targetState.segments.findIndex(
   585→        s => s.type === 'user_question' && s.questionData?.questionId === questionId
   586→      );
   587→      if (segIdx !== -1) {
   588→        targetState.segments[segIdx] = {
   589→          ...targetState.segments[segIdx],
   590→          userAnswer: answers,
   591→          userFreeText: freeText,
   592→        };
   593→        targetState.messages = targetState.messages.map(m =>
   594→          m.id === targetState!.assistantId
   595→            ? { ...m, segments: [...targetState!.segments] }
   596→            : m
   597→        );
   598→        if (activeConversationIdRef.current === targetConvId) {
   599→          setMessages(targetState.messages);
   600→        }
   601→      }
   602→    }
   603→
   604→    // POST to backend (unchanged)
   605→    try {
   606→      await fetch('/api/chat/respond', {
   607→        method: 'POST',
   608→        headers: {
   609→          'Content-Type': 'application/json',
   610→          ...(userSessionId ? { 'X-Session-ID': userSessionId } : {}),
   611→        },
   612→        body: JSON.stringify({
   613→          question_id: questionId,
   614→          answers,
   615→          free_text: freeText || null,
   616→        }),
   617→      });
   618→    } catch (e) {
   619→      console.error('Failed to respond to question:', e);
   620→    }
   621→  },
   622→  [userSessionId]
   623→);
   624→```
   625→
   626→**Step 3: Refactor deleteMessage**
   627→
   628→Change guard from `if (isStreaming) return;` to check per-conversation:
   629→
   630→```typescript
   631→const deleteMessage = useCallback(
   632→  (messageIndex: number) => {
   633→    const convId = activeConversationIdRef.current;
   634→    // Only block if this specific conversation is streaming
   635→    if (convId && streamStatesRef.current.has(convId)) return;
   636→    // ... rest unchanged
   637→  },
   638→  []
   639→);
   640→```
   641→
   642→**Step 4: Remove old singleton refs that are no longer referenced**
   643→
   644→Remove `abortRef` if not used anywhere else. Verify no references to `streamGenerationRef`, `streamConversationIdRef`, `assistantIdRef`, `segmentsRef`, `currentTextRef`, `textBufferRef`, `flushTimerRef`, `phaseRef` remain.
   645→

   647→
   648→Run: `cd frontend && npx tsc --noEmit 2>&1 | head -30`
   649→Expected: PASS (or only warnings)

   652→

   655→git commit -m "feat: refactor editMessage/deleteMessage/respondToQuestion for per-stream state"

## Summary: Core AgentContext Refactor (Tasks 1-5)

**File modified:** `/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/.claude/worktrees/implement-memory/frontend/src/contexts/AgentContext.tsx`

**Commit:** `26970818` on branch `worktree-implement-memory`

### What changed

**1. Added `StreamState` interface** (lines 14-24) containing all per-stream state: `assistantId`, `segments`, `currentText`, `textBuffer`, `phase`, `abortController`, `sessionId`, `messages`, `flushTimer`.

**2. Replaced 9 singleton refs with 2 new state holders** (lines 50-52):
- `streamStatesRef` -- a `Map<string, StreamState>` keyed by conversation ID
- `streamingConversationIds` -- a React state `Set<string>` for UI indicators

Removed refs: `textBufferRef`, `flushTimerRef`, `assistantIdRef`, `segmentsRef`, `currentTextRef`, `phaseRef`, `streamConversationIdRef`, `streamGenerationRef`, `abortRef`.

Kept refs: `sessionIdRef`, `pendingHistoryRef`, `messagesRef`, `messagesCacheRef`, `activeConversationIdRef`.

**3. Replaced `flushText` with `flushTextForStream`** (lines 54-69) -- operates on a `StreamState` object and only updates React state when the conversation is currently active.

**4. Rewrote `sendMessage`** (lines 71-369):
- Per-conversation guard instead of global `isStreaming` guard
- Creates `StreamState` per conversation, stores in map
- All callbacks close over the `StreamState` object (no singleton refs)
- Helper functions: `updateMessages`, `scheduleFlush`, `flushNow`, `cleanupStream`
- `onDone`/`onError` clean up the stream map and cache messages for background-completed streams

**5. Rewrote `editMessage`** (lines 371-671):
- Aborts any existing stream for the conversation before starting a new one
- Same per-stream callback pattern as `sendMessage`
- Uses `runAgentEditLoop` with fresh session

**6. Refactored `deleteMessage`** (lines 673-703):
- Per-conversation streaming guard: `if (convId && streamStatesRef.current.has(convId)) return`
- Removed dependency on `isStreaming` in the dependency array (now `[]`)

**7. Refactored `respondToQuestion`** (lines 705-760):
- Searches all active streams in `streamStatesRef` to find the one containing the question
- Updates that stream's segments and messages
- Only updates React state if the stream's conversation is active

**8. Rewrote `loadMessages`** (lines 762-802):
- Stream re-attachment: if incoming conversation has an active stream, reads `streamState.messages` directly
- Only caches outgoing conversation if it is NOT streaming (streaming state lives in `streamStatesRef`)

**9. Rewrote `clearMessages`** (lines 804-820):
- Same non-streaming cache logic as `loadMessages`
- Simplified: no more complex segment capture/finalization

**10. Provider value** (line 824): passes `streamingConversationIds` in addition to existing values (for Task 6 to expose via the interface).

### Verification

- TypeScript compilation: **zero errors** (`npx tsc --noEmit` passes cleanly)
- The `buildChatMessages` import was removed since the old stale-generation re-fetch logic is no longer needed
- All 9 removed singleton refs have zero remaining references in the file
- The `streamingConversationIds` is passed in the provider value but not yet in the context interface (Task 6 will update `useAgent.ts` to expose it)

It compiles without error. This is because TypeScript allows passing extra properties in the `value` prop to `Context.Provider` -- the Provider accepts an object that is a supertype. The `createContext<AgentContextValue>` type only constrains what consumers can _read_, not what producers can _write_. So the extra `streamingConversationIds` in the value is silently accepted but **inaccessible to consumers** since the type doesn't declare it.

This means **consumers cannot access `streamingConversationIds`** from `useAgent()` without a type assertion. This is a real issue for Task 7 (sidebar streaming indicator).

**ChatInput.tsx** (line 17): Still uses `const { sendMessage, isStreaming } = useAgent();` -- does NOT destructure `streamingConversationIds`. The spec says to add it, but since the existing `isStreaming` still works for the active conversation guard, the ChatInput behavior is functionally correct. However, it does not match the spec.

## Code Review: AgentContext Per-Conversation StreamState Refactor (Tasks 1-5)

### What was done well

The refactor successfully replaces a fragile singleton-ref architecture with a clean per-conversation `StreamState` map. The core insight -- that each conversation's SSE stream should own its own mutable state object, and callbacks should close over that object -- is architecturally sound. Key positives:

- **StreamState interface** (`/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/.claude/worktrees/implement-memory/frontend/src/contexts/AgentContext.tsx`, line 14-24) is well-defined and captures all the per-stream mutable state that was previously spread across 8 separate singleton refs.
- **All old singleton refs fully removed**: `abortRef`, `textBufferRef`, `flushTimerRef`, `assistantIdRef`, `segmentsRef`, `currentTextRef`, `phaseRef`, `streamConversationIdRef`, and `streamGenerationRef` are completely gone. No dead references remain.
- **`updateMessages` helper pattern** (lines 142-147, 453-458) is clean -- it always updates `state.messages` and conditionally updates React state only when the user is viewing that conversation.
- **loadMessages re-attachment** (lines 776-784) is simple and correct: if an active stream exists for the incoming conversation, it reads `streamState.messages` directly, giving instant re-attachment.
- **TypeScript compiles cleanly** with zero errors.
- **Removed `buildChatMessages` import** since the stale-generation re-fetch logic (the `setTimeout(() => fetch(...)`) in the old `onDone`/`onError`) is no longer needed -- each stream writes to its own state and cleanup is deterministic.
- **`editMessage` properly aborts** an existing stream before starting a new one (lines 376-388), which is the correct behavior for an edit operation.

### Plan Alignment Analysis

**Deviation 1: Single commit instead of incremental commits per task.**
The plan called for separate commits for Tasks 1-5 (one per task). The implementation used a single commit (`26970818 feat: refactor AgentContext to per-conversation StreamState map`). This is a **non-issue** -- the incremental commits were primarily for risk reduction during development. The final result is functionally equivalent.

**Deviation 2: `streamingConversationIds` added to Provider value early.**
The plan assigned exposing `streamingConversationIds` in the context to Task 6. The implementation already passes it in the Provider value (line 824) even though the `AgentContextValue` interface in `useAgent.ts` has not been updated. This is technically harmless (TypeScript allows extra properties), but it means the value is currently inaccessible to consumers. This is a **minor forward-leak** -- not a bug, but the interface update must happen in Task 6 for this to be useful.

**Deviation 3: `respondToQuestion` does not accept a `conversationId` parameter.**
The plan (Task 5 Step 2) specified adding a `conversationId` parameter:
```
async (questionId: string, answers: string[], freeText?: string, conversationId?: string | null)
```
The implementation (line 706) omits this parameter:
```
async (questionId: string, answers: string[], freeText?: string)
```
Instead it searches all active streams (lines 712-718). This is a **justified departure** -- the search-all-streams approach is more robust and does not require callers to know which conversation a question belongs to.

---

### Issues

#### Important (should fix)

**1. Non-null assertion `convId!` when `convId` can be `null`.**

In `sendMessage`, `convId` is defined as `conversationId || null` (line 73). When `convId` is `null`, the stream state is still created and callbacks still fire, but `scheduleFlush` and `flushNow` call `flushTextForStream(state, convId!)` (lines 153, 165). This passes `null` cast to `string` into `flushTextForStream`, which then compares `activeConversationIdRef.current === conversationId` (line 66). Since `activeConversationIdRef.current` could also be `null`, this comparison would be `null === null` -- which is `true` -- and would call `setMessages`. So it **happens to work** for the null case, but the `!` assertion is misleading and fragile.

The same pattern appears in `editMessage` (lines 464, 476), though `convId` there comes from `activeConversationIdRef.current` which is also nullable.

**Recommendation**: Either guard against null before calling `flushTextForStream`, or change the `conversationId` parameter type in `flushTextForStream` to `string | null` so the intent is explicit. Example fix:

```typescript
const flushTextForStream = useCallback((state: StreamState, conversationId: string | null) => {
```

This makes the null-equality comparison intentional rather than accidental.

**2. When `convId` is `null`, the stream state is never stored in `streamStatesRef` and never cleaned up via `cleanupStream`.**

In `sendMessage` lines 116-119:
```typescript
if (convId) {
  streamStatesRef.current.set(convId, state);
  setStreamingConversationIds(prev => new Set(prev).add(convId));
}
```

And `cleanupStream` lines 174-181:
```typescript
if (convId) {
  streamStatesRef.current.delete(convId);
  ...
}
```

If `sendMessage` is called without a `conversationId`, the `StreamState` object is created but **never registered** in `streamStatesRef`. This means:
- No per-conversation guard prevents double-sends (line 75 is `if (convId && ...)`).
- The state object's `flushTimer` is never guaranteed to be cleaned on error paths.
- `streamingConversationIds` will never reflect this stream.

This path seems to represent the "no conversation yet" case (first message before backend creates a conversation). It works because the stream operates on React state directly (activeConversationIdRef is `null` which matches `convId` being `null`), and there is no concurrency concern. However, **if the `flushTimer` fires after the stream completes and after cleanup, it would call `flushTextForStream` on a stale state**. The `cleanupStream` function does clear the timer, but only when `convId` is truthy. When `convId` is `null`, `cleanupStream` clears the timer but does nothing else, which is actually fine.

**Recommendation**: This is acceptable for now but worth adding a comment at lines 116-119 explaining that null-convId streams are inherently single-conversation (the "new chat" path) and do not need map registration.

**3. `editMessage` reads from `messagesRef.current` but should read from `state.messages` of the existing stream (if any).**

In `editMessage` (line 403):
```typescript
const currentMsgs = messagesRef.current;
```

If the user quickly edits a message while the conversation is streaming (and the abort just happened on line 378), `messagesRef.current` reflects React state which may be one render behind the aborted stream's `state.messages`. Since the abort happened synchronously and the code immediately reads `messagesRef`, this is a timing edge case. In practice, the `setMessages(streamMessages)` on line 449 overwrites whatever was there, so the truncation logic on line 428 (`currentMsgs.slice(0, messageIndex)`) should be safe because `messagesRef` was synced via the `useEffect` on line 44.

**Recommendation**: This is low risk but worth noting. The `useEffect` sync is deferred, so immediately after aborting a stream whose callbacks mutated `state.messages` but did not yet call `setMessages`, `messagesRef.current` could be stale by one flush. Consider reading from the aborted stream's `state.messages` if it existed:

```typescript
const currentMsgs = existingState ? existingState.messages : messagesRef.current;
```

(Move the `existingState` variable to be accessible after the abort block.)

**4. `onDone` calls `cleanupStream()` before the conditional cache write.**

In `sendMessage`'s `onDone` (lines 316-322):
```typescript
cleanupStream();  // deletes state from map
if (activeConversationIdRef.current === convId) {
  setIsStreaming(false);
} else if (convId) {
  // Cache final messages so switching back shows completed response
  messagesCacheRef.current.set(convId, state.messages);
}
```

`cleanupStream()` deletes the `StreamState` from `streamStatesRef` on line 175. Then on line 321, `state.messages` is read. This works because `state` is a local variable that the closure captured -- deleting it from the map does not invalidate the object. However, there is a subtle ordering concern: after `cleanupStream` removes the state from the map, if `loadMessages` is called concurrently (e.g., user switches conversations in the same event loop tick), it will not find the stream state and will not attempt re-attachment. But the cache write on line 321 has not happened yet. This is a negligible race in practice because React batches state updates and user actions are sequential.

**Recommendation**: Consider swapping the order -- cache the messages first, then clean up the stream state:

```typescript
if (convId && activeConversationIdRef.current !== convId) {
  messagesCacheRef.current.set(convId, state.messages);
}
cleanupStream();
if (activeConversationIdRef.current === convId) {
  setIsStreaming(false);
}
```

#### Suggestions (nice to have)

**5. Significant callback code duplication between `sendMessage` and `editMessage`.**

The callback objects for `sendMessage` (lines 190-362) and `editMessage` (lines 499-664) are nearly identical -- approximately 170 lines duplicated. The only differences are:
- `editMessage` does not dispatch `skills-updated` / `memories-updated` events in `onToolResult`.
- `editMessage` uses `runAgentEditLoop` instead of `runAgentLoop`.

**Recommendation**: Extract a factory function that builds the callbacks object given a `state`, `convId`, `assistantId`, and optional event dispatch flags. This would reduce the file by approximately 150 lines and ensure future callback changes are applied consistently. For example:

```typescript
function buildStreamCallbacks(
  state: StreamState,
  convId: string | null,
  assistantId: string,
  opts: { dispatchToolEvents?: boolean }
): StreamCallbacks { ... }
```

**6. `loadMessages` deletes the cache entry on line 794 even when it was used.**

```typescript
messagesCacheRef.current.delete(incoming);
```

This is correct behavior (consume the cache), but if the user switches away and back rapidly without the backend having persisted the response, the cache is gone. The new architecture handles this better than before (via `streamStatesRef` re-attachment), so this is fine -- just noting it for completeness.

**7. The `respondToQuestion` signature does not match the `useAgent.ts` interface exactly.**

The interface declares:
```typescript
respondToQuestion: (questionId: string, answers: string[], freeText?: string) => void;
```
The return type is `void`, but the implementation returns `Promise<void>`. TypeScript allows this since `Promise<void>` is assignable to `void` in this context, but it means callers cannot `await` the result through the context. This pre-dates this refactor and is not a regression.

**8. No abort mechanism exposed for background streams.**

The old code had `abortRef` which was set per-stream but at least accessible. Now each `StreamState` contains its own `abortController`, but there is no way for consumers to abort a background stream (e.g., a "stop generating" button for a non-active conversation). This is a future concern if stop/cancel UI is planned for background streams.

### Summary

The refactor is well-executed and faithfully implements the planned architecture from Tasks 1-5. The core architectural change -- from singleton refs with a generation counter to a per-conversation `StreamState` map -- is a clear improvement that enables simultaneous streams and eliminates the fragile stale-generation detection logic. The code compiles cleanly and the patterns are internally consistent.

The most actionable items are:
1. Fix the `convId!` non-null assertions to use an explicit `string | null` type (Important).
2. Extract shared callback logic between `sendMessage` and `editMessage` to reduce ~150 lines of duplication (Suggestion, but high value for maintainability).
3. Consider reordering `cleanupStream` vs. cache write in `onDone`/`onError` to close a theoretical race window (Important, low effort).

---

### Fix 1: `flushTextForStream` signature accepts `string | null`

**Line 54** -- Changed the `conversationId` parameter type from `string` to `string | null`:

```typescript
const flushTextForStream = useCallback((state: StreamState, conversationId: string | null) => {
```

Then removed the non-null assertion operator (`!`) from all 4 call sites where `flushTextForStream` was invoked with `convId!`:
- `sendMessage` > `scheduleFlush` (line 153): `flushTextForStream(state, convId)` 
- `sendMessage` > `flushNow` (line 165): `flushTextForStream(state, convId)`
- `editMessage` > `scheduleFlush` (line 467): `flushTextForStream(state, convId)`
- `editMessage` > `flushNow` (line 479): `flushTextForStream(state, convId)`

This makes the null-equality comparison inside `flushTextForStream` intentionally correct when `convId` is null (the "new chat before conversation is created" path).

---

### Fix 2: Cache write before `cleanupStream()` in `onDone` and `onError`

In all 4 callback locations (`onDone` and `onError` in both `sendMessage` and `editMessage`), reordered so the cache write happens **before** `cleanupStream()`:

**Before:**
```typescript
cleanupStream();
if (activeConversationIdRef.current === convId) {
  setIsStreaming(false);
} else if (convId) {
  messagesCacheRef.current.set(convId, state.messages);
}
```

**After:**
```typescript
if (convId && activeConversationIdRef.current !== convId) {
  messagesCacheRef.current.set(convId, state.messages);
}
cleanupStream();
if (activeConversationIdRef.current === convId) {
  setIsStreaming(false);
}
```

This closes a theoretical race window where `cleanupStream()` deletes the stream state from the map before the cache write has a chance to persist the final messages.

---

### Fix 3: Use existing stream's messages in `editMessage`

In `editMessage`, before aborting an existing stream, the code now saves a reference to the existing stream's messages. Those messages are then used (when available) instead of `messagesRef.current` for building the truncated message list.

**Added** `existingMessages` variable before the abort block:
```typescript
let existingMessages: ChatMessage[] | null = null;
if (convId && streamStatesRef.current.has(convId)) {
  const existingState = streamStatesRef.current.get(convId)!;
  existingMessages = existingState.messages;
  existingState.abortController.abort();
  // ...
}
```

**Changed** the message source line from:
```typescript
const currentMsgs = messagesRef.current;
```
to:
```typescript
const currentMsgs = existingMessages || messagesRef.current;
```

This ensures that when editing mid-stream, the truncation is based on the stream's latest message state rather than the potentially stale React state ref.
