I now have all the information needed to provide a thorough review. Let me compile my findings.

---

## Code Review: Live Cross-Conversation Streaming Implementation

### Overview

This review covers the complete implementation of per-conversation streaming architecture across 4 commits (26970818..0bf0e03f), replacing the generation-counter-based singleton approach with a `StreamState` map. The implementation touches 7 frontend files as planned, with no backend changes.

**What was done well:**

- The core architectural shift from singleton refs to a per-conversation `StreamState` map is clean and well-structured. The `StreamState` interface at `/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/.claude/worktrees/implement-memory/frontend/src/contexts/AgentContext.tsx` (lines 14-24) captures all necessary per-stream state.
- The `updateMessages` helper pattern (lines 142-147) that conditionally pushes to React state only when the conversation is active is elegant and avoids unnecessary re-renders.
- The `cleanupStream` helper (lines 169-182) properly clears both the map entry and the reactive `streamingConversationIds` set.
- The `loadMessages` re-attachment logic (lines 782-789) is straightforward -- just read from `streamStatesRef` and set `isStreaming(true)`.
- TypeScript compiles cleanly with zero errors.
- The CSS pulse animation (ConversationHistory.css lines 134-149) uses theme variables with a sensible fallback.
- Prop threading through App -> Sidebar -> ConversationHistory is done correctly without prop drilling issues.

---

### Critical Issues (Must Fix)

**1. `sendMessage` null-conversation race condition -- messages sent without a conversationId bypass stream state entirely**

In `/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/.claude/worktrees/implement-memory/frontend/src/contexts/AgentContext.tsx`, line 73-75:

```typescript
const convId = conversationId || null;
// Per-conversation guard: don't double-send in same conversation
if (convId && streamStatesRef.current.has(convId)) return;
```

And lines 116-119:

```typescript
if (convId) {
  streamStatesRef.current.set(convId, state);
  setStreamingConversationIds(prev => new Set(prev).add(convId));
}
```

When `convId` is `null`, the `StreamState` is never stored in the map. This means: (a) the per-conversation guard is bypassed -- the user can send multiple messages with no conversationId, (b) no streaming indicator appears in the sidebar, and (c) the `cleanupStream` function does nothing (line 174: `if (convId) { ... }`). Looking at the calling code in `ChatInput.tsx` (lines 56-61), a `convId` is always created via `createConversation` before `sendMessage` is called, so in practice this path should not be hit in normal use. However, this is a defensive gap -- if `createConversation` somehow fails or a caller invokes `sendMessage` without a conversationId, the stream runs entirely outside the state map with no guard, no indicator, and no cleanup. The old code had `if (isStreaming) return;` as a global guard, which at least prevented double-sends.

**Recommendation:** Either assert that `convId` is always non-null at the entry point of `sendMessage`, or add a fallback guard such as:

```typescript
if (!convId && isStreaming) return;
```

This preserves backward compatibility for the edge case.

**2. `activeConversationIdRef.current === convId` comparison is true when both are null**

In `/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/.claude/worktrees/implement-memory/frontend/src/contexts/AgentContext.tsx`, line 122:

```typescript
if (activeConversationIdRef.current === convId) {
  setMessages(streamMessages);
  setIsStreaming(true);
}
```

When both `activeConversationIdRef.current` and `convId` are `null`, this comparison evaluates to `true`, so `setMessages` and `setIsStreaming` are called. This is correct for the initial "no conversation selected" state, but combined with issue 1 above, it means the `flushTextForStream` callback on line 66 (`if (activeConversationIdRef.current === conversationId)`) will also fire when both are null. This effectively falls back to the old singleton behavior for the null-convId case -- workable, but undocumented and fragile.

---

### Important Issues (Should Fix)

**3. `editMessage` skips `create_skill` and `save_memory`/`forget_memory` event dispatches**

In `/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/.claude/worktrees/implement-memory/frontend/src/contexts/AgentContext.tsx`, the `editMessage` `onToolResult` callback (lines 538-566) does not dispatch `skills-updated` or `memories-updated` custom events, while `sendMessage` does (lines 254-259). This was also missing in the original code, so it is not a regression, but since this was a major rewrite, it would have been a good time to address it.

**4. `respondToQuestion` does not accept a `conversationId` parameter as planned**

The plan (Task 5, Step 2) specified adding a `conversationId` parameter to `respondToQuestion`:

```typescript
async (questionId: string, answers: string[], freeText?: string, conversationId?: string | null) => {
```

The implementation at `/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/.claude/worktrees/implement-memory/frontend/src/contexts/AgentContext.tsx` line 711 only has:

```typescript
async (questionId: string, answers: string[], freeText?: string) => {
```

And the interface in `/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/.claude/worktrees/implement-memory/frontend/src/hooks/useAgent.ts` line 13 also omits it:

```typescript
respondToQuestion: (questionId: string, answers: string[], freeText?: string) => void;
```

The current implementation compensates by searching all active streams (lines 717-723), which works but is O(n) across all streams. This is acceptable for a small number of concurrent conversations, but deviates from the plan. This deviation is not problematic on its own since `questionId` should be unique across streams, but it means callers cannot optimize by providing the conversation context.

**5. Massive code duplication between `sendMessage` and `editMessage`**

The `editMessage` function (lines 372-676) duplicates virtually all callback bodies from `sendMessage` (lines 71-370). Both define identical `updateMessages`, `scheduleFlush`, `flushNow`, and `cleanupStream` helpers, and the 10 SSE callbacks (`onTextChunk`, `onThinkingDone`, `onToolCall`, `onToolResult`, `onSubagentStart`, `onSubagentEnd`, `onDone`, `onError`, `onUserQuestion`) are copy-pasted with minimal differences. This is approximately 300 lines of duplicated logic.

**Recommendation:** Extract the callback creation and helper functions into a shared factory function, e.g.:

```typescript
function createStreamCallbacks(
  state: StreamState,
  convId: string | null,
  assistantId: string,
  ...
): AgentCallbacks { ... }
```

This would reduce the file from 835 lines to approximately 550 lines and make future changes less error-prone (currently, a bug fix in one set of callbacks could easily be missed in the other).

**6. `onDone` caches messages before `cleanupStream` deletes the stream entry -- potential ordering issue**

In `/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/.claude/worktrees/implement-memory/frontend/src/contexts/AgentContext.tsx`, `sendMessage`'s `onDone` (lines 299-323):

```typescript
onDone: (newSessionId) => {
  // ...
  updateMessages(msgs => ...);      // line 309
  if (convId && activeConversationIdRef.current !== convId) {
    messagesCacheRef.current.set(convId, state.messages);  // line 317
  }
  cleanupStream();                   // line 319 -- deletes from streamStatesRef
  if (activeConversationIdRef.current === convId) {
    setIsStreaming(false);           // line 321
  }
},
```

The `cleanupStream()` call on line 319 calls `setStreamingConversationIds` which triggers a re-render. If a `loadMessages` call is dispatched during that re-render (e.g., the user clicks a conversation at exactly the right moment), `loadMessages` (line 774) checks `streamStatesRef.current.has(outgoing)` -- but the stream was already deleted by `cleanupStream`. This means the outgoing conversation's messages might not be cached properly. The window is very small, but the ordering is not ideal. The cache write on line 317 partially mitigates this, but only for background (non-active) conversations.

**Recommendation:** Move `cleanupStream()` after `setIsStreaming(false)` to ensure all state updates happen before the stream entry is removed from the map. Or better, move the cache write into `cleanupStream` itself.

---

### Suggestions (Nice to Have)

**7. CSS variable name inconsistency**

In `/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/.claude/worktrees/implement-memory/frontend/src/components/ConversationHistory.css`, line 139:

```css
background: var(--accent-color, #3b82f6);
```

The rest of the CSS uses `--color-accent-primary` (e.g., line 84). The `--accent-color` variable may not be defined, causing it to always fall back to `#3b82f6`. Consider using `var(--color-accent-primary, #3b82f6)` for consistency.

**8. `streamingConversationIds` creates a new `Set` on every state update**

In `/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/.claude/worktrees/implement-memory/frontend/src/contexts/AgentContext.tsx`, line 118:

```typescript
setStreamingConversationIds(prev => new Set(prev).add(convId));
```

This is fine for correctness (React needs referential inequality to trigger re-renders), but every component receiving this Set via context will re-render on every set change even if they do not care about the specific conversation that changed. For the current use case (sidebar indicator), this is acceptable since the sidebar already re-renders for other reasons. No action needed now, but if performance becomes a concern, consider memoizing a derived boolean per component.

**9. `loadMessages` does not reset `sessionIdRef` when re-attaching to a streaming conversation**

In `/Users/cyyeh/Desktop/duckdb-data-agent/.claude/worktrees/implement-memory/frontend/src/contexts/AgentContext.tsx`, lines 782-789:

```typescript
if (streamState) {
  // Re-attach: use live stream messages
  setMessages(streamState.messages);
  setIsStreaming(true);
  return;  // Early return -- sessionIdRef is NOT reset
}
```

When re-attaching to a streaming conversation, `sessionIdRef` retains whatever value it had from the outgoing conversation. When the stream eventually completes, `onDone` sets `sessionIdRef.current = newSessionId` (line 302), which is correct. But if the user sends a message in the re-attached conversation before it finishes (currently blocked by the per-conversation guard), the stale `sessionIdRef` would not cause problems. Still, for clarity, consider setting `sessionIdRef.current = streamState.sessionId` during re-attachment.

**10. `buildChatMessages` import removed but utility file still exists**

The old code imported `buildChatMessages` from `../utils/buildChatMessages` in AgentContext. This import was correctly removed since `loadMessages` no longer calls the stale-generation refetch pattern. However, `buildChatMessages` is still used in `ConversationContext.tsx` (line 3 and line 59), so the utility file is still needed. This is not an issue, just confirming the import removal was correct.

---

### Plan Alignment Summary

| Plan Task | Status | Notes |
|-----------|--------|-------|
| Task 1: StreamState type + streamStatesRef | Complete | Matches plan exactly |
| Task 2: flushTextForStream | Complete | Matches plan exactly |
| Task 3: sendMessage rewrite | Complete | Matches plan; `onDone`/`onError` ordering slightly different (cache before cleanup vs. plan's cleanup first) -- this is actually better |
| Task 4: loadMessages/clearMessages re-attachment | Complete | Matches plan; re-attachment logic is clean |
| Task 5: editMessage/deleteMessage/respondToQuestion | Partial | `respondToQuestion` missing `conversationId` param per plan; `editMessage` missing skill/memory event dispatches (not a regression) |
| Task 6: Expose streamingConversationIds | Complete | Matches plan; ChatInput unchanged (correctly not needed) |
| Task 7: Sidebar streaming indicator | Complete | Matches plan; CSS variable name differs from convention |
| Task 8: Build verification | Complete | TypeScript passes with 0 errors |

### Overall Assessment

The implementation successfully replaces the singleton generation-counter-based streaming architecture with a per-conversation StreamState map. The core design is sound and achieves the stated goals: multiple simultaneous streams, live re-attachment on conversation switch, and sidebar streaming indicators. The code is well-structured within each function, and the TypeScript types are correct.

The primary area for improvement is the significant code duplication between `sendMessage` and `editMessage` (~300 lines). The null-conversationId edge case (Critical Issue 1) should be addressed to prevent potential double-send bugs, though in practice the current calling convention always provides a conversationId. The CSS variable inconsistency (Suggestion 7) is a minor polish item that should be fixed to maintain visual consistency across themes.
